# Parity ledger

Generated by `cargo xtask parity` from `parity/parity.toml`. Do not edit this file by hand.

Every documented upstream behavior appears here exactly once, with the status `PORTED` or `NOT PORTED`. There is no third status.

| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Fallbacks | Fallbacks | Below every search, Tinycast shows a few rows under Use "…" with. Each one sends what you typed somewhere else. They always appear at the bottom, below the real results. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |

| Fallbacks | Fallbacks | Quick AI — Sent as a question in the palette — AI is on. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Fallbacks | Fallbacks | Run Shell Command — Runs in zsh, with its output in a window — Always, unless you turn it off. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Fallbacks | Fallbacks | Search Files — Opens file search with it typed — File Search is on. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |

| Fallbacks | Fallbacks › The built-in fallbacks | This is separate from custom commands. Turning off custom commands doesn't hide it; only its own checkbox in Settings does. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Fallbacks | Fallbacks › The built-in fallbacks | Your text runs in /bin/zsh from your home folder with your shell configuration loaded, so your own aliases like ll work. A window shows the output as it's printed, with Stop and Run Again buttons. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |

### System actions — `docs/launcher/system-actions/`

| Feature | Sub-feature | Behavior | Status | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| System actions | System actions | Eject All Disks ejects external and removable drives, including a hard drive connected through a dock. It never ejects internal or network volumes. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| System actions | System actions | Toggle Microphone Mute changes the default input device's own mute setting. If your microphone doesn't support software mute, or its mute control is managed externally, Tinycast explains why it couldn't change it. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| System actions | System actions | Toggle System Appearance changes the appearance of macOS itself, not only Tinycast. Tinycast follows the change only while its own theme is set to System. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| System actions | System actions | Volume. Turn Volume Up and Turn Volume Down move in 5% steps: from 37%, up goes to 40% and down goes to 35%. Tinycast shows its own volume indicator, because macOS only shows one for the hardware media keys. It shows the level as a number, shows Muted instead of 0%, and fades out after 1.6 seconds. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| System actions | System actions | When you run Hide All Apps Except Frontmost or Quit All Applications from a shortcut with the palette closed, they act on the app that's actually in front. Quit All Applications leaves Finder and Tinycast running and asks apps to quit normally, so apps with unsaved work still ask you to save. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |

### Uninstall an app — `docs/launcher/uninstall/`

| Feature | Sub-feature | Behavior | Status | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Uninstall an app | Uninstall an app | Bundle identifier. An exact match, or a name inside the app's own namespace. com.apple.iBooksX.CacheDelete belongs to com.apple.iBooksX, but com.apple.iBooksXtra doesn't. A dash also counts as a separator, so dev.zed.Zed-Preview.plist belongs to Zed, unless Zed Preview is also installed, in which case it belongs to Zed Preview. A short vendor prefix like com.adobe never claims everything under it. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Uninstall an app | Uninstall an app | Command-line tools in /usr/local/bin, /opt/homebrew/bin, ~/.local/bin and ~/bin belong to the app only if they link into the app bundle, never because of their name. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Uninstall an app | Uninstall an app | Display name. This is the weakest rule, so rows matched this way are labeled "matched by name" and you can check them before you confirm. The name must match exactly, ignoring case and accents, and never as a prefix or part of a word, so "Books" and "Books Reader" can't claim each other's folders. The name also needs at least 3 characters, can't be a standard Library folder name like Preferences or Caches, and can't be shared with another installed app. This rule only applies in Application Support, Caches, Logs and plug-in folders. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Uninstall an app | Uninstall an app | Group container. Tinycast removes the group. prefix and the 10-character team ID, then applies the rule above. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Uninstall an app | Uninstall an app | Tinycast uses four rules, from most to least certain. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Uninstall an app | Uninstall an app | For example, without Full Disk Access, ~/Library/Containers, ~/Library/Group Containers and ~/Library/Cookies are locked, but ~/Library/Application Scripts next to them isn't. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Uninstall an app | Uninstall an app | Tinycast checks whether it has Full Disk Access but never asks for it. See Permissions. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Uninstall an app | Uninstall an app | /usr/local/bin/code stays locked because that folder belongs to the system. Removing it would require an administrator password, and the uninstaller never asks for one. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Uninstall an app | Uninstall an app | You can't select a locked row. From most to least important, a row is locked when the file is missing, protected by the system, locked by you in Get Info, only accessible with Full Disk Access, in a folder you can't write to, or owned by another user. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Uninstall an app | Uninstall an app | If the app is running, Tinycast quits it first. Then everything you selected moves to the Trash, with the app itself last, so if something fails you can run the uninstaller again. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Uninstall an app | Uninstall an app | The app's shortcut, favorite, visibility and learned ranking are removed only if the app itself was removed. If you only cleaned up leftover files, the app and its settings stay. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Uninstall an app | Uninstall an app | A ≥ before a size means measuring stopped at its limit. Sizes match what Finder shows, so Xcode shows 9.45 GB, not the 4.19 GB its compressed files use on disk. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Uninstall an app | Uninstall an app | If you confirm within the first second, the total shown can be lower than what actually moves to the Trash. Everything you selected is still moved; the number just wasn't finished yet. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Uninstall an app | Uninstall an app | The list appears right away, and sizes fill in afterward. A row that's still being measured shows a blank size. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Uninstall an app | Uninstall an app | Everything is moved to the Trash, and nothing is deleted permanently. If the uninstaller picks the wrong file, you can drag it back. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Uninstall an app | Uninstall an app | Remove an app along with the caches, preferences and containers it leaves behind. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Uninstall an app | Uninstall an app | When you drag an app to the Trash, its support files stay behind in your Library folder. Tinycast's uninstaller finds those files and removes them along with the app. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Uninstall an app | Uninstall an app | A screen opens listing the app and every file that belongs to it, each with a checkbox. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Uninstall an app | Uninstall an app | Click a checkbox or double-click a row to select or deselect it. The search field filters by name or location. Copy Path keeps the screen open, so you don't have to scan again. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Uninstall an app | Uninstall an app | Copy Path — ⌥⌘C. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Uninstall an app | Uninstall an app | Return always asks you to confirm first, whether you use the key or the menu. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Uninstall an app | Uninstall an app | Select an app in the launcher, press ⌘K and choose Uninstall Application. There's no keyboard shortcut for it, so you can't start it by accident. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Uninstall an app | Uninstall an app | Select / Unselect File — ⌘return. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Uninstall an app | Uninstall an app | Show in Finder — ⇧⌘O. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Uninstall an app | Uninstall an app | Show Info in Finder — ⇧⌘I. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Uninstall an app | Uninstall an app | Uninstall Application — return. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Uninstall an app | Uninstall an app | Any of your document folders | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Uninstall an app | Uninstall an app | **Anything directly in your home folder.** For example, VS Code's bundle is named `Code`, and | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Uninstall an app | Uninstall an app | Anything more than one level deep in a folder it scans | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Uninstall an app | Uninstall an app | Many people keep their source code in ~/Code. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Uninstall an app | Uninstall an app | `/private/var/db/receipts`, `~/Library/Keychains` and `/Library/Extensions` | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Uninstall an app | Uninstall an app | Tinycast itself | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |

### Calculator — `docs/features/calculator/`

| Feature | Sub-feature | Behavior | Status | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Calculator | Calculator | 10k is 10,000. Scientific notation works (1e5, 5e-3km, 3e+2), while 2e and 2 e mean 2 × Euler's e. Right after a currency, M, bn and T also work: $2M / 4 is 500,000.00 USD. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Calculator | Calculator | 4(2+3) is 20. 2pi, 2sqrt(9) and (2+3)(2+3) all work, and 6/2(1+2) gives the same answer as 6/2(1+2). A lone x between numbers also multiplies, so 3x3 and $5 x 2 work. Two numbers separated only by a space never multiply, so 5 3 stays a search. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Calculator | Calculator | A trailing operator keeps the last answer on screen. 10 + shows 10, and 10kg + 500g + shows 10,500 g, so the card doesn't flicker while you type the next number. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Calculator | Calculator | Spoken roots work with measurements: square root of 25m2 is 5 m. root 4 of 81 is 3, and log 81 base 3 is 4. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Calculator | Calculator | Thousand, million, billion, trillion and quadrillion scale the number before them: 2 trillion is 2,000,000,000,000. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Calculator | Calculator | Use mod for the remainder, because % already means percent. It has the same precedence as multiply and divide, and keeps the sign of the first number: -10 mod 3 is -1. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Calculator | Calculator | You can also write the operators as words: 10 plus 5, 10 minus 3, 6 times 7, 10 divided by 2, 10 multiplied by 3 and 2 to the power of 10. 3 raised to 4, 2 exponent 10 and 3 to the 4 are powers too. 4 squared is 16 and 3 cubed is 27. per means divide, so 60 miles per hour is 60 mi/hr. percent, per cent and percentage mean %, so 20 percent of 50 is 10. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Calculator | Calculator | Calculator History is a separate screen that you open with the command of the same name. It isn't part of the tab cycle. To leave it, press esc, or delete in an empty search. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |

| Hotkeys | Hotkeys | Press 🎤 (F5) while recording any shortcut to use it in Tinycast, push-to-talk included. While a shortcut uses it, macOS Dictation doesn't; remove the shortcut to give it back. File Search, Notes, AI, Quick Actions, Navigation, Calendar, window commands, quicklinks, snippets, custom commands and extensions all check their switch before doing anything. Turn the feature back on and its shortcuts work again. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |

| Hotkeys | Hotkeys › Hyper key | The option is dimmed while Hyper Key is set to None. Changing the physical Hyper key resets Quick Press to Does Nothing. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Hotkeys | Hotkeys › Hyper key | Many people who use Caps Lock as their Hyper key choose Escape. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Hotkeys | Hotkeys › Hyper key | Settings → General → Quick Press sets what happens when you press Caps Lock by itself while it's your Hyper key: Does Nothing (default), Trigger Caps Lock, or Trigger Escape. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Hotkeys | Hotkeys › Hyper key | Password fields, password managers and some terminals turn on macOS Secure Input, which hides every key press from apps like Tinycast. While it's on, the Hyper key can't work, and pressing it shows a message saying why. Diagnostics names the app most likely holding Secure Input. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Hotkeys | Hotkeys › Hyper key | With Caps Lock as your Hyper key, turn on Secure Input Compatibility to keep Tinycast's own Hyper shortcuts working there. Caps Lock then acts as Right Control, so while it's on, the Right Control key also works as Hyper and its own shortcuts are reserved. Inside Secure Input, Right Control plus a shortcut's key runs that Hyper shortcut. Left Control never triggers Hyper; existing Tinycast Control shortcuts still work with Left Control. The Control combinations used by Hyper are reserved while Secure Input lasts. Other apps' Hyper shortcuts still don't work there. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Hotkeys | Hotkeys › Hyper key | The Status row under the key shows a dot: green while the Hyper key works. Click Diagnostics… to see what's running, which keyboards are remapped, and anything that conflicts. Restart Hyper Key sets everything up again. If another app takes over a Caps Lock remapping, Status shows Taken Over and identifies the affected keyboards. Tinycast leaves that app's mapping in place. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Hotkeys | Hotkeys › Hyper key | Any shortcut that includes all the Hyper modifiers is shown as a single ✦. For example, ⌃⌥⌘G is shown as ✦G, or ✦⇧G if it uses an extra modifier. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Hotkeys | Hotkeys › Hyper key | This only changes how the shortcut is displayed. With no Hyper key set, ⌃⌥⌘G is shown as it is. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |

### Backup & restore — `docs/reference/backup/`

| Feature | Sub-feature | Behavior | Status | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Backup & restore | Backup & restore | **AI** and **MCP servers**: agreeing to send text to a model and run server code | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Backup & restore | Backup & restore | **Auto Join Meetings** and **Camera Preview**: agreeing to open links and turn on the camera | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Backup & restore | Backup & restore | **Calendar**: agreeing to let Tinycast read your calendar | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Backup & restore | Backup & restore | **Dictation**: agreeing to let Tinycast use your microphone | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Backup & restore | Backup & restore | **Extensions**: agreeing to run third-party code | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Backup & restore | Backup & restore | **Fallback order and checkboxes**: these could add a shell command runner to your launcher | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Backup & restore | Backup & restore | **Quick Actions**: agreeing to let Tinycast type into other apps | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Backup & restore | Backup & restore | **Search text in images and PDFs**: agreeing to text recognition in the background | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Backup & restore | Backup & restore | **Snippets**: agreeing to keyword matching | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Backup & restore | Backup & restore | Some switches are never included in a backup, because turning them on means agreeing to something, not just setting a preference: | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Backup & restore | Backup & restore | **Sync settings file**: agreeing to let a file on this Mac change your settings | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Backup & restore | Backup & restore | This means a backup someone sends you can't turn on keystroke listening, run third-party code, read your calendar, record from your microphone or contact an AI provider. You turn those features on yourself, in the app, after reading what they do. Importing snippets doesn't turn snippets on. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Backup & restore | Backup & restore | A backup is a single .tinycast file. You choose what goes into it when you export, and separately choose what to restore when you import. So a backup that contains everything can still restore only your snippets. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Backup & restore | Backup & restore | Clipboard History — Text and image clips with their images, and references to copied files. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Backup & restore | Backup & restore | Launcher Learning — Your learned launcher ranking, plus emoji and calculator history. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Backup & restore | Backup & restore | Notes — Your note Markdown files. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Backup & restore | Backup & restore | Save your setup to one file, choose what to export and what to import, and what a backup never does. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Backup & restore | Backup & restore | Settings → Backup. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Backup & restore | Backup & restore | Settings & Shortcuts — Shortcuts, custom commands, quicklinks, window layouts, rooms, custom window sizes, favorites, aliases, preferences. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Backup & restore | Backup & restore | Snippets — Your snippet Markdown files. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Backup & restore | Backup & restore | The launcher also has Export Backup and Import Backup commands. They always include everything, because there's no room for checkboxes in the launcher. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Backup & restore | Backup & restore | Copied files in clipboard history are backed up as paths, not file contents. When you import, entries for files that don't exist on the new Mac are skipped. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Backup & restore | Backup & restore | Every import shows a summary of what changed, by category. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Backup & restore | Backup & restore | If the file contains custom commands, Tinycast warns you before adding them, because a file that adds shell commands without telling you is a security risk. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Backup & restore | Backup & restore | Imports add to what you have instead of overwriting it. Snippets and notes are added next to your existing ones, and a note with a name that's already taken gets a number added instead of replacing yours. Importing the same file twice doesn't create duplicates. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Backup & restore | Backup & restore | Launcher Learning is the exception. It replaces your existing data, because combining the habits of two Macs wouldn't match either one. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Backup & restore | Backup & restore | AI chats, API keys, and all AI and MCP settings — Conversations and keys stay on the Mac where they were created. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Backup & restore | Backup & restore | Cached data, like currency rates and update checks — Tinycast downloads it again as needed. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Backup & restore | Backup & restore | Clipboard images are stored inside the backup by name, not by their location on your Mac. The only paths a backup includes are the locations of copied files, because that location is what the entry records. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Backup & restore | Backup & restore | Dictation settings, its shortcut and downloaded models — They belong to this Mac's microphone and disk. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Backup & restore | Backup & restore | Extension package manager and custom search paths — They're specific to this Mac, not part of your setup. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Backup & restore | Backup & restore | Extensions: their code, data and sign-ins — They're third-party code, and their sign-ins are in the Keychain. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Backup & restore | Backup & restore | Launch at login and Show in menu bar are read from their current state, so they're restored the way you had them. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Backup & restore | Backup & restore | Palette position, auto-switch input source, calendar checkboxes, the browser for meeting links, Snippets and Notes folders — They name things on this Mac that another Mac may not have. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Backup & restore | Backup & restore | Quick Actions model, prompts, language and custom actions — An import should never change what a shortcut does to your text. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Backup & restore | Backup & restore | The backup format can change between versions of Tinycast. A backup is only guaranteed to import into the same version that created it. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Backup & restore | Backup & restore | The file records which version created it. If your version of Tinycast doesn't recognize the format, it tells you instead of importing part of the file. Use backups to move your setup to another Mac or to restore after reinstalling, not as a long-term archive. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Backup & restore | Backup & restore | If the file already exists when you turn sync on, Tinycast asks whether to Import it or Replace it with your current settings. A value Tinycast can't use keeps the current one, and a message tells you what was wrong. Turning sync off leaves the file where it is. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Backup & restore | Backup & restore | Like a backup, the file can't turn on Snippets, Extensions, Calendar, Auto Join Meetings, Camera Preview, Quick Actions, MCP servers, Dictation or text recognition. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Backup & restore | Backup & restore | The file holds your preferences, the shortcuts and aliases of apps, System Settings panes, system actions and built-in commands, and all of window management, including custom sizes, layouts and rooms. It never holds your snippets, notes, quicklinks, custom commands, MCP servers or AI connections. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Backup & restore | Backup & restore | The Sync settings file switch in Settings → Backup → Settings File keeps a copy of your settings in ~/.config/tinycast/settings.json. It's off by default. The beta uses ~/.config/tinycast-beta/. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Backup & restore | Backup & restore | While it's on, a change you make in Settings is written to the file, and an edit you save in the file applies in Tinycast right away. If the file is a symlink, it stays one, so it can live in a dotfiles repository. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Backup & restore | Backup & restore | AI chats — ai-chats.sqlite3, never included in a backup. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Backup & restore | Backup & restore | Clipboard history — clipboard.sqlite3, with images next to it. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Backup & restore | Backup & restore | Everything a backup includes is also a regular file. By default, it's in ~/Library/Application Support/com.tinycast.app/: | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Backup & restore | Backup & restore | Notes — Notes/, or the Notes Folder you chose. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Backup & restore | Backup & restore | Quicklinks — quicklinks.sqlite3, with its own JSON export. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Backup & restore | Backup & restore | Snippets and notes are plain Markdown files. Copying those folders works as a backup, and you can read the files without Tinycast. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Backup & restore | Backup & restore | Snippets — Snippets/, or the Snippets Folder you chose. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |

| Import from Raycast | Import from Raycast | See Installing extensions. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Import from Raycast | Import from Raycast | Two things aren't part of a .rayconfig, so they have their own importers: | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Import from Raycast | Import from Raycast | Importing at least one quicklink turns on the Quicklinks feature. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |

### Updates — `docs/reference/updates/`

| Feature | Sub-feature | Behavior | Status | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Updates | Updates | The stable version only updates to stable releases, and the beta only to beta releases. They're separate apps, and an update never switches you from one to the other. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Updates | Updates | Brew install, brew uninstall and brew list work as usual. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Updates | Updates | Tinycast's Homebrew casks tell Homebrew that the app updates itself, so brew upgrade skips Tinycast. It never reports Tinycast as outdated, never downloads it again, and never rolls back a copy that updated itself. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Updates | Updates | Check for Updates always looks for a newer version, including one you skipped. It's in the launcher, the Tinycast menu bar menu and Settings → About. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Updates | Updates | If you choose Later, Tinycast stops offering that version. It still offers newer ones. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Updates | Updates | **Once a day**, Tinycast checks GitHub for a newer release on its channel. If a check fails, it | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Updates | Updates | One click downloads it, with a progress bar and a **Cancel** button that stops the download. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Updates | Updates | The first time the new version opens, it shows Updated to Tinycast and the version number. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Updates | Updates | Tinycast verifies the download, installs it and offers to **relaunch**. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Updates | Updates | Tries again two hours later. The first check runs 30 seconds after launch, so it doesn't slow down login. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Updates | Updates | When there's a new release, a window shows **what changed**, taken from the release notes. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Updates | Updates | Each version is offered at most once per launch. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Updates | Updates | The update window never interrupts you. It waits while the palette is open, a snippet is expanding, an extension command is running, the uninstaller is working, you're recording a shortcut, or a dialog is open. It tries again every two minutes for half an hour, then goes back to checking once a day. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Updates | Updates | /Applications, it tells you instead of working around it. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Updates | Updates | Nothing is installed unless every check passes. If any check fails, the app you're running stays exactly as it was. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Updates | Updates | Running. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |

| Extensions | Extensions | Only one foreground command runs at a time, and it keeps a JavaScript engine in memory until you leave it. Menu bar commands use their own short-lived engine, which is released when a refresh finishes or the menu closes. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Extensions | Extensions | Starting a command stops the previous one and discards its engine, so nothing carries over between runs. Once warmed up, a command starts in about 7 ms. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Extensions | Extensions | The only exception is background refresh, which briefly runs a command on a schedule, and only if you turn it on. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |

Its icon and title then stay in the menu bar, and the interval in its manifest controls how often it refreshes in the background. Opening the menu reloads the command, and closing it releases the engine once any running action finishes. Saved items come back after a restart without running the extension. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| Extensions | Extensions | To stop a menu bar command, turn off Show in menu bar in the command's configuration. Installing an extension doesn't turn on its menu bar commands. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |

### What works — `docs/extensions/compatibility/`

| Feature | Sub-feature | Behavior | Status | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| What works | What works | 32 of 37 extensions, and 114 of 147 view commands, open and render. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| What works | What works | We tested Tinycast against the 37 extensions installed in Raycast on the development Mac: | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| What works | What works | A WebSocket to a server macOS doesn't trust — Certificate checks can't be turned off, so the connection fails. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| What works | What works | AI, BrowserExtension, WindowManagement — These are Raycast services with no local equivalent. Using one fails with a clear message. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| What works | What works | AI tools (tools/) — Not shown. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| What works | What works | Canceling a fetch in progress — The caller gets its AbortError, but the request still finishes in the background. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| What works | What works | If an extension needs something that's missing, it tells you when you run it instead of failing silently or showing a broken screen. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| What works | What works | Net and tls — Load without errors, but fail when used. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| What works | What works | Streaming HTTP — Responses arrive all at once, so server-sent events and download progress don't work. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| What works | What works | Writing to a running childprocess.spawn — Input is sent once, when the command starts. Later writes are dropped. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| What works | What works | APIs. Clipboard, LocalStorage, Cache, environment, getPreferenceValues, showToast, showHUD, confirmAlert, closeMainWindow, popToRoot, clearSearchBar, open, trash, showInFinder, getApplications, getDefaultApplication, getFrontmostApplication, getSelectedText, getSelectedFinderItems, launchCommand, updateCommandMetadata, openExtensionPreferences, OAuth, useNavigation, Icon, Color, Image.Mask, Keyboard.Shortcut.Common, LaunchType. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| What works | What works | Bundled packages. Extensions that ship their own nodemodules load those packages, and createRequire, WebAssembly and SharedArrayBuffer work. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| What works | What works | Bundled Swift helpers, like Color Picker's, run normally. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| What works | What works | Command modes. view commands appear in the palette. no-view commands run in the background with the palette closed. A no-view command with an interval can refresh on a schedule; see Background refresh. menu-bar commands show native menu items and refresh on their manifest interval, without keeping JavaScript in memory between runs; see Menu bar commands. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| What works | What works | Components. List and Grid with sections, empty views, item details and search bar dropdowns. Detail with Metadata. Form with every field type: text, password, text area, checkbox, dropdown, tag picker, date picker, file picker, separator and description. ActionPanel with sections and submenus, and Action with all its built-in variants. Older names still used by published extensions also work. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| What works | What works | Http and https requests use the same code as fetch, so libraries like axios and node-fetch work. HTTP over a Unix socket works too, which is how Docker's extension reaches its daemon. Streams are fully implemented, so pipelines like fetch → file work from start to finish. childprocess.spawn streams the command's output as it runs. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| What works | What works | Node built-ins. path, fs and fs/promises, os, childprocess, crypto, zlib, http and https, stream, util, events, buffer, url, querystring, punycode, assert, stringdecoder and timers. Other built-ins load without errors and only fail when they're actually used. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| What works | What works | Raycast:// links are handled inside Tinycast. Tinycast registers this link type; if Raycast is also installed, macOS decides which app receives those links. A link to an installed extension command runs that command, whether it comes from another app, the browser or an extension, with its arguments, fallbackText, launchType and context applied. tinycast:// links work the same way. A Raycast Store Install link opens Search extensions on that extension. Any other link reopens the palette, since passing it on would open Raycast itself. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| What works | What works | Sign-in with OAuth. Extensions like GitHub sign you in through your browser, and the palette comes back when you finish. Sign in from one of the extension's commands first: a background refresh or menu bar item can't start a sign-in on its own. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| What works | What works | WebSockets. The WebSocket global and the bundled ws package both connect, so extensions like Home Assistant get live updates. Names ending in .local, like homeassistant.local, resolve through macOS. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |
| What works | What works | What Tinycast supports from the Raycast API, how well it works in practice, and the known gaps. | NOT PORTED | Not yet implemented. |

| `docs/ai/mcp/` | 2 | `ai-mcp.limits.use-25-by-default-or-10-50-100-or-unlimited` | Use: 25 by default, or 10, 50, 100 or Unlimited. A model that keeps calling tools without answering is stuck, so when it reaches the limit, the reply ends with a note saying how many rounds it used. With Codex, the limit counts individual tool calls instead of rounds, which stops a runaway reply sooner. With Unlimited, a reply only ends when the model finishes, when you click Stop, or, on an API connection, when the tool history passes 1 MB. The reply keeps running while the palette is hidden or the window is closed, and every round is billed by your provider or counted against your plan. | Not yet implemented. |

| `docs/features/calculator/` | 1 | `features-calculator.units.results-use-base-units-unless-you-name-a-uni` | Results use base units unless you name a unit with to, in or as. A rate multiplied by time keeps the rate's own unit, so 10 l/min 30 s is 5 L. Power multiplied by minutes or hours gives Wh or kWh, and current multiplied by minutes or hours gives Ah or mAh. m² and m2 both mean square meters, while (2m)^2 squares the whole amount. Use pi (2m)^2 for the area of a circle. | Not yet implemented. |

An app's global shortcut uses the running app or macOS's default copy. | `docs/launcher/uninstall/` | 1 | `launcher-uninstall.how-files-are-matched-to-the-app.bundle-identifier-an-exact-match-or-a-name-i` | Bundle identifier. An exact match, or a name inside the app's own namespace. com.apple.iBooksX.CacheDelete belongs to com.apple.iBooksX, but com.apple.iBooksXtra doesn't. A dash also counts as a separator, so dev.zed.Zed-Preview.plist belongs to Zed, unless Zed Preview is also installed, in which case it belongs to Zed Preview. A short vendor prefix like com.adobe never claims everything under it. | Not yet implemented. |
| `docs/launcher/uninstall/` | 1 | `launcher-uninstall.how-files-are-matched-to-the-app.command-line-tools-in-usr-local-bin-opt-home` | Command-line tools in /usr/local/bin, /opt/homebrew/bin, ~/.local/bin and ~/bin belong to the app only if they link into the app bundle, never because of their name. | Not yet implemented. |
| `docs/launcher/uninstall/` | 1 | `launcher-uninstall.how-files-are-matched-to-the-app.display-name-this-is-the-weakest-rule-so-row` | Display name. This is the weakest rule, so rows matched this way are labeled "matched by name" and you can check them before you confirm. The name must match exactly, ignoring case and accents, and never as a prefix or part of a word, so "Books" and "Books Reader" can't claim each other's folders. The name also needs at least 3 characters, can't be a standard Library folder name like Preferences or Caches, and can't be shared with another installed app. This rule only applies in Application Support, Caches, Logs and plug-in folders. | Not yet implemented. |
| `docs/launcher/uninstall/` | 1 | `launcher-uninstall.how-files-are-matched-to-the-app.group-container-tinycast-removes-the-group-p` | Group container. Tinycast removes the group. prefix and the 10-character team ID, then applies the rule above. | Not yet implemented. |
| `docs/launcher/uninstall/` | 1 | `launcher-uninstall.how-files-are-matched-to-the-app.tinycast-uses-four-rules-from-most-to-least` | Tinycast uses four rules, from most to least certain. | Not yet implemented. |
| `docs/launcher/uninstall/` | 1 | `launcher-uninstall.locked-rows.for-example-without-full-disk-access-library` | For example, without Full Disk Access, ~/Library/Containers, ~/Library/Group Containers and ~/Library/Cookies are locked, but ~/Library/Application Scripts next to them isn't. | Not yet implemented. |
| `docs/launcher/uninstall/` | 1 | `launcher-uninstall.locked-rows.tinycast-checks-whether-it-has-full-disk-acc` | Tinycast checks whether it has Full Disk Access but never asks for it. See Permissions. | Not yet implemented. |
| `docs/launcher/uninstall/` | 1 | `launcher-uninstall.locked-rows.usr-local-bin-code-stays-locked-because-that` | /usr/local/bin/code stays locked because that folder belongs to the system. ## 4. Windows additions

Windows-only behaviors that are the natural counterpart of an upstream behavior. They never count toward parity and never replace an upstream behavior.

No Windows additions have been recorded yet.

## 5. Environment note

Evidence in this ledger was produced on Windows 11 (x86_64), with the Rust toolchain pinned in `rust-toolchain.toml`.

Rows marked `PORTED` were proven by the evidence named in the row. Rows marked `NOT PORTED` are either impossible on Windows or implemented but not yet verified against real Windows behavior; the reason column says which.
